  "old_string": "\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Design principles for escaping SCB.}\nThree conditions appear necessary for escaping the symbolic mirage. (i)~\\emph{Structural independence}: the verifier must share no architecture or training data with the generator. Our debate control achieves $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\\,pp), showing that within-model diversity comes at the cost of quality. (iii)~\\emph{Task-verifier alignment}: the verification paradigm must match task structure. The path forward lies in structurally independent verification, guided by $K_\\text{eff}$, BR, and $C_0$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:extended-discussion}).",
  "new_string": "\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Design principles for escaping SCB.}\nThree conditions appear necessary for escaping the symbolic mirage. (i)~\\emph{Structural independence}: the verifier must share no architecture or training data with the generator (debate control: $\\kappa = 0.15$ but $-18.6$\\,pp accuracy collapse). (ii)~\\emph{Positive capability gap}: the verifier's standalone accuracy must exceed the generator's SC; combo gain decreases monotonically across four generators (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli})---only Mistral-7B (SC below verifier) gains significantly. (iii)~\\emph{Task-verifier alignment}: all three tested NLI architectures produce gains at $w \\geq 3$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:capability-gap}); generalization beyond propositional-logic NLI remains open. The path forward lies in structurally independent verification, guided by $K_\\text{eff}$, BR, and $C_0$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:extended-discussion})."
}

(i)~\\emph{Structural independence}: the verifier must share no architecture or training data with the generator. Our debate control achieves $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\\,pp), showing that within-model diversity comes at the cost of quality. (ii)~\\emph{Positive capability gap}: the verifier's standalone accuracy must exceed the generator's SC. The monotonic decrease in combo gain across four generators (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}) confirms this threshold---only Mistral-7B (SC below verifier) gains significantly. (iii)~\\emph{Task-verifier alignment}: the verification paradigm must match task structure. All three tested NLI architectures produce gains at $w \\geq 3$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:capability-gap}), indicating robustness across verifier choice within the NLI paradigm; whether this generalizes beyond propositional-logic NLI remains open. The path forward lies in structurally independent verification, guided by $K_\\text{eff}$, BR, and $C_0$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:extended-discussion}).\n",
    "structuredPatch": [
      {

        "oldLines": 4,

(ii)~\\emph{Positive capability gap}: the verifier's standalone accuracy must exceed the generator's SC. The monotonic decrease in combo gain across four generators (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}) confirms this threshold---only Mistral-7B (SC below verifier) gains significantly. (iii)~\\emph{Task-verifier alignment}: the verification paradigm must match task structure. All three tested NLI architectures produce gains at $w \\geq 3$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:capability-gap}), indicating robustness across verifier choice within the NLI paradigm; whether this generalizes beyond propositional-logic NLI remains open. The path forward lies in structurally independent verification, guided by $K_\\text{eff}$, BR, and $C_0$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:extended-discussion}).",

    "originalFile": "\\enlargethispage{8\\baselineskip}\n\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\n\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Practitioner decision framework.}\nThree zero-cost diagnostics predict self-verification failure: $K_\\text{eff} \\leq 1.3$, SC $< 40\\%$ ($-2.83$\\,pp), and $C_0 < 5\\%$ (degenerates to majority voting). Only the intermediate regime (SC $\\sim$50--60\\%, $K_\\text{eff} > 1.8$) offers marginal gain.\n\n\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{SCB beyond SICA.}\nSCB extends to any method extracting structured representations from a model's outputs---self-verification \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect}, self-refinement \\citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, and chain-of-verification \\citep{dhuliawala2023chainofverification} all face the same distributional prior.\n\n\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Design principles for escaping SCB.}\nThree conditions appear necessary for escaping the symbolic mirage. (i)~\\emph{Structural independence}: the verifier must share no architecture or training data with the generator (debate control: $\\kappa = 0.15$ but $-18.6$\\,pp accuracy collapse). (ii)~\\emph{Positive capability gap}: the verifier's standalone accuracy must exceed the generator's SC; combo gain decreases monotonically across four generators (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli})---only Mistral-7B (SC below verifier) gains significantly. (iii)~\\emph{Task-verifier alignment}: all three tested NLI architectures produce gains at $w \\geq 3$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:capability-gap}); generalization beyond propositional-logic NLI remains open. ```text
1	\enlargethispage{8\baselineskip}
2	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
3	\label{sec:discussion}
4	\label{sec:conclusion}
5	
6	\looseness=-1
7	\paragraph{Practitioner decision framework.}
8	Three zero-cost diagnostics predict self-verification failure: $K_\text{eff} \leq 1.3$, SC $< 40\%$ ($-2.83$\,pp), and $C_0 < 5\%$ (degenerates to majority voting). Only the intermediate regime (SC $\sim$50--60\%, $K_\text{eff} > 1.8$) offers marginal gain.
9	
10	\looseness=-1
11	\paragraph{SCB beyond SICA.}
12	SCB extends to any method extracting structured representations from a model's outputs---self-verification \citep{huang2024selfcorrect}, self-refinement \citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, and chain-of-verification \citep{dhuliawala2023chainofverification} all face the same distributional prior.
13	
14	\looseness=-1
15	\paragraph{Design principles for escaping SCB.}
16	Three conditions appear necessary for escaping the symbolic mirage. (i)~\emph{Structural independence}: the verifier must share no architecture or training data with the generator (debate control: $\kappa = 0.15$ but $-18.6$\,pp accuracy collapse). (ii)~\emph{Positive capability gap}: the verifier's standalone accuracy must exceed the generator's SC; combo gain decreases monotonically across four generators (Table~\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli})---only Mistral-7B (SC below verifier) gains significantly. (iii)~\emph{Task-verifier alignment}: all three tested NLI architectures produce gains at $w \geq 3$ (Appendix~\ref{app:capability-gap}); generalization beyond propositional-logic NLI remains open (Appendix~\ref{app:extended-discussion}).
17	
```

[Director 指令] [紧急 Patch — Table 3 数据修正 + 叙事升级]

## 1. Table 3 数据修正（critical）

solutions.tex Table 3 (tab:cross_generator_nli) LLaMA-8B 行数据错误。Reviewer 核实确认。

修正：
```
LLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 70.00\% & $-$15.00 & $+$4.17\,pp & 0.004 \\
```

原错误行：
```
LLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\,pp & 0.66 \\
```

**同时核查 Qwen2.5-14B 行**：Reviewer 提示可能存在 73.50% vs 74.00% 差异。当前写的是 74.00%，如果 exp-091 registry 显示 73.50%，改为 73.50%。

## 2. 叙事升级（major）

修正后，cross-architecture escape 证据从"仅 Mistral-7B 显著"升级为"Mistral-7B 和 LLaMA-8B 均显著"。涉及以下修改：

### §6 Cross-Architecture (solutions.tex)

**Main result 段落**修改：
- 旧："On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+6.67$\,pp ($p < 0.001$)"
- 新：增加 LLaMA 结果。类似："On ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), combo $w{=}3$ achieves significant gains for both Mistral-7B ($+6.67$\,pp, $p < 0.001$) and LLaMA-8B ($+4.17$\,pp, $p = 0.004$), while stronger generators show no improvement."

**Capability gap 段落**修改：
- 旧："only Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\%, below the verifier) gains significantly"
- 新：纳入 LLaMA-8B (SC 70.00%, ABOVE the verifier yet still gains). 关键措辞：combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability, with significance observed for generators up to SC ≈ 70\% but not above 74\%.
- 注意：DeBERTa standalone=55% < LLaMA SC=70%，但 combo 仍然显著 → "positive capability gap" 条件需要修正为更细致的表述：combo effectiveness depends on the generator-verifier gap magnitude, not simply on whether the verifier exceeds the generator.

**Table 3 caption**修改：
- 旧："Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability ($w{=}3$)"
- 新："Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability; gains are significant for generators with SC $\leq 70\%$ ($w{=}3$)."

### §7 Discussion design principles

修正第二条 (positive capability gap)：
- 旧（如果还是简单的 "verifier exceeds generator"）→ 新："The combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability (Table~\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}), remaining significant for generators up to SC $\approx 70\%$ but vanishing above $74\%$. Per-label analysis reveals the mechanism: NLI and SC excel on complementary labels, with complementarity diminishing as the generator improves across all labels."

### §6 "Why it works" 段落

当前指向 §7 design principles。确保与上述修改一致。可以保留 preliminary 措辞但增加 LLaMA 数据作为支撑。

### Abstract / Introduction

如果有提及 "仅在 Mistral-7B 上显著" 的内容，更新为包含 LLaMA-8B。检查 abstract 中是否有 cross-architecture gain 的数值——如果有，可以更新为 "+4.17--6.67pp (p ≤ 0.004)"。

## 3. BH 校正确认

增加 LLaMA-8B 后，BH 校正的 m 值可能需要更新。当前 §6 说 "After BH correction ($m{=}12$), only Mistral-7B ProofWriter conditions ($w{=}3$, $w{=}5$) remain significant." 

LLaMA-8B w=3 p=0.004，在 BH m=12 下 adjusted p = 0.004 × 12/rank。如果 rank=2 或 3，adjusted p ≈ 0.012-0.024，仍然显著。请更新 BH 校正结果，确认 LLaMA-8B 是否通过校正。

## 优先级
这个修正比之前所有修改更重要——直接影响论文核心 cross-architecture claim 的强度。请优先处理。

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01Sjh3GxsmBwFBQudQoDRnNW`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}
2	\label{sec:solutions}
3	\label{sec:cross-arch}
4	
5	\paragraph{Method.}
6	\looseness=-1 We test with DeBERTa-large \citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. This proof-of-concept is limited to propositional-logic benchmarks whose premise-conclusion structure naturally suits NLI; generalization to non-logical domains remains untested. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$).
7	
8	\paragraph{Main result.}
9	\looseness=-1 DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\%) and 55.00\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo $w{=}3$ yields $+2.94$\,pp (n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+6.67$\,pp ($p < 0.001$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+11.00$\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp; Table~\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\S\ref{sec:mirage}). After BH correction ($m{=}12$), only Mistral-7B ProofWriter conditions ($w{=}3$, $w{=}5$) remain significant.
10	
11	\paragraph{Why it works.}
12	\looseness=-1 Our preliminary results suggest three conditions jointly enable the escape---structural independence, positive capability gap, and task-verifier alignment---expanded in \S\ref{sec:discussion}. NLI classification operates on (premise, conclusion) pairs, a different task from constraint extraction; the success may partly reflect task-format alignment. Per-label analysis confirms NLI and SC excel on complementary labels (Appendix~\ref{app:multi-nli}); all three NLI architectures produce gains at $w \geq 3$.
13	
14	\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}
15	\looseness=-1 Four generators spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\% standalone; Table~\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}): only Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\%, below the verifier) gains significantly ($+6.67$\,pp, $p < 0.001$); stronger generators show no improvement. The monotonic relationship holds across three NLI architectures and six verifier-generator pairs (Figure~\ref{fig:capability-gap}; Appendix~\ref{app:capability-gap}). A $2{\times}2$ generator${\times}$extractor matrix confirms BR $> 1$ in all eight cells ($1.94$--$2.70$; Appendix~\ref{app:2x2}).
16	
17	\begin{table}[t]
18	\centering
19	\small
20	\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability ($w{=}3$).}
21	\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}
22	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}
23	\toprule
24	Generator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\
25	\midrule
26	Mistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\,pp & $<$0.001 \\
27	LLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\,pp & 0.66 \\
28	Qwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\,pp & 0.50 \\
29	Qwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\,pp & 0.45 \\
30	\bottomrule
31	\end{tabular}}
32	\end{table}
33	
34	\paragraph{Disagreement-Routed Cascade.}
35	A cost-aware variant invokes NLI only below consensus threshold $\tau$, recovering 90\% of full-combo gain at ${\sim}$35\% NLI cost ($\tau{=}0.7$; Appendix~\ref{app:drc}).
36	
```

A $2{\\times}2$ generator${\\times}$extractor matrix confirms BR $> 1$ in all eight cells ($1.94$--$2.70$; Appendix~\\ref{app:2x2}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability ($w{=}3$).}\n\\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\\,pp & $<$0.001 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 70.00\\% & $-$15.00 & $+$4.17\\,pp & 0.004 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\\,pp & 0.50 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\\,pp & 0.45 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Disagreement-Routed Cascade.}\nA cost-aware variant invokes NLI only below consensus threshold $\\tau$, recovering 90\\% of full-combo gain at ${\\sim}$35\\% NLI cost ($\\tau{=}0.7$; Appendix~\\ref{app:drc}).\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}\n\\label{sec:solutions}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\n\\paragraph{Method.}\n\\looseness=-1 We test with DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. This proof-of-concept is limited to propositional-logic benchmarks whose premise-conclusion structure naturally suits NLI; generalization to non-logical domains remains untested. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$).\n\n\\paragraph{Main result.}\n\\looseness=-1 DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\\%) and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\\%). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+6.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+11.00$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). After BH correction ($m{=}12$), only Mistral-7B ProofWriter conditions ($w{=}3$, $w{=}5$) remain significant.\n\n\\paragraph{Why it works.}\n\\looseness=-1 Our preliminary results suggest three conditions jointly enable the escape---structural independence, positive capability gap, and task-verifier alignment---expanded in \\S\\ref{sec:discussion}. NLI classification operates on (premise, conclusion) pairs, a different task from constraint extraction; the success may partly reflect task-format alignment. Per-label analysis confirms NLI and SC excel on complementary labels (Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli}); all three NLI architectures produce gains at $w \\geq 3$.\n\n\\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}\n\\looseness=-1 Four generators spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\\% standalone; Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}): only Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\\%, below the verifier) gains significantly ($+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$); stronger generators show no improvement. The monotonic relationship holds across three NLI architectures and six verifier-generator pairs (Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}; Appendix~\\ref{app:capability-gap}). A $2{\\times}2$ generator${\\times}$extractor matrix confirms BR $> 1$ in all eight cells ($1.94$--$2.70$; Appendix~\\ref{app:2x2}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability; gains are significant for generators with SC $\\leq 70\\%$ ($w{=}3$).}\n\\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\\,pp & $<$0.001 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 70.00\\% & $-$15.00 & $+$4.17\\,pp & 0.004 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 73.50\\% & $-$18.50 & $+$0.83\\,pp & 0.405 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\\,pp & 0.45 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Disagreement-Routed Cascade.}\nA cost-aware variant invokes NLI only below consensus threshold $\\tau$, recovering 90\\% of full-combo gain at ${\\sim}$35\\% NLI cost ($\\tau{=}0.7$; Appendix~\\ref{app:drc}).\n",

1	\enlargethispage{8\baselineskip}
2	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
3	\label{sec:discussion}
4	\label{sec:conclusion}
5	
6	\looseness=-1
7	\paragraph{Practitioner decision framework.}
8	Three zero-cost diagnostics predict self-verification failure: $K_\text{eff} \leq 1.3$, SC $< 40\%$ ($-2.83$\,pp), and $C_0 < 5\%$ (degenerates to majority voting). Only the intermediate regime (SC $\sim$50--60\%, $K_\text{eff} > 1.8$) offers marginal gain.
9	
10	\looseness=-1
11	\paragraph{SCB beyond SICA.}
12	SCB extends to any method extracting structured representations from a model's outputs---self-verification \citep{huang2024selfcorrect}, self-refinement \citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, and chain-of-verification \citep{dhuliawala2023chainofverification} all face the same distributional prior.
13	
14	\looseness=-1
15	\paragraph{Design principles for escaping SCB.}
16	Three conditions appear necessary for escaping the symbolic mirage. (i)~\emph{Structural independence}: the verifier must not share architecture or training data with the generator (debate: $\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\,pp). (ii)~\emph{Positive capability gap}: the verifier's standalone accuracy must exceed the generator's SC; combo gain decreases monotonically across four generators (Table~\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli})---only Mistral-7B (SC below verifier) gains. (iii)~\emph{Task-verifier alignment}: all three NLI architectures produce gains at $w \geq 3$ (Appendix~\ref{app:capability-gap}); generalization beyond propositional-logic NLI remains open (Appendix~\ref{app:extended-discussion}).
17	
```

### Tool result

_source line 22427 · 2026-05-22T14:36:21.032Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01PYoh6X5tDyECCyG8MVduet`</summary>

```text
1	Self-Consistency (SC) majority voting over chain-of-thought traces saturates as sample count grows because same-model traces are correlated voters.
2	We investigate whether symbolic constraint extraction can break this plateau through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT.
3	Across six open-source LLMs (7B--32B) on three logical reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\sim}$1,250 unique instances), we find no evidence of improvement (aggregate sign test: 5+/10$-$, $p = 0.30$; none of sixteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant positive effect after FDR control), with equivalence established in four conditions, significant degradation in two, and ten remaining underpowered for effects below ${\sim}$2 percentage points.
4	We trace the failure to \emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (SCB): on incorrect problems, 83.3\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratios ranging from $1.66\times$ to $4.97\times$), but a shuffle control shows this bias is structural---it reflects trace-count distributions rather than extraction-specific error ($\text{BR}_\text{obs} \approx \text{BR}_\text{null}$, $p > 0.7$). MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on 94.4\% of problems, confirming that the formal verification layer adds no information beyond majority voting despite 99\% of constraints passing Z3 validation.
5	Cross-model extraction confirms SCB universality across nine models from 7B to frontier (BR $1.66$--$4.97\times$), though frontier models use cross-model rather than self-extraction.
6	Twenty-seven training-free remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all fail, confirming a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off.
7	Oracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\,pp, $+14.22$\,pp FOLIO, $+17.17$\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$); same-model self-extraction captures only ${\sim}$12\% of this ceiling.
8	Cross-architecture NLI verification on propositional-logic benchmarks escapes the bottleneck when the verifier exceeds the generator ($+6.67$\,pp, $p < 0.001$), with gain monotonically ordered by the capability gap ($p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$).
9	
```

</details>

_source line 22431 · 2026-05-22T14:36:25.541Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01YHzG1vx7xybCxrwTWdsqft`</summary>

```text
25	
26	Oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\,pp Mistral-7B, $+14.22$\,pp Qwen-14B on FOLIO, $p < 0.001$; \S\ref{sec:oracle}), yet self-extraction captures only ${\sim}$12\% of this ceiling.
27	The central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off \citep{krogh1994neural}: Debate reduces $\kappa$ to 0.15 but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\,pp); other methods cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$, a same-model ceiling across three families (\S\ref{sec:remediation}).
28	A cross-architecture proof-of-concept escapes the bottleneck: DeBERTa NLI yields $+6.67$\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
29	
30	Our contributions are:
31	\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]
32	\item \textbf{Quantifying symbolic confirmation bias in formal self-verification.} While the general difficulty of LLM self-correction is known \citep{huang2024selfcorrect}, we provide the first quantitative characterization of symbolic confirmation bias in formal self-verification, demonstrating that provably sound verification machinery becomes informationally vacuous under self-extraction: 83.3\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong answer (BR $1.66$--$4.97\times$ across nine models), yet 99\% pass Z3 validation. A shuffle control and MAX-SAT ablation show this bias is structural---the formal layer adds no information beyond majority voting. Twenty-seven training-free strategies fail to break it; cross-model extraction confirms universality across nine models from 7B to frontier (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).
33	
34	\item \textbf{Diagnostic toolkit for independence-accuracy trade-offs.} We adapt the known diversity--accuracy trade-off \citep{krogh1994neural} to LLM self-extraction, contributing three practical diagnostics ($K_\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$) that predict when symbolic aggregation will fail. Same-model $\kappa \geq 0.40$ across four methods and three families; cross-architecture NLI yields $+6.67$\,pp ($p < 0.001$; $w{=}3$), with gain monotonically ordered by the capability gap ($p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; \S\ref{sec:remediation}, \S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
35	
36	\item \textbf{Process-level independence analysis.} CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) exceeds answer-level $\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$) across four models from three families; under cross-model extraction on frontier models, SICA gain decreases monotonically with process similarity ($\rho = -1.0$, $p = 0.008$; \S\ref{sec:process-kappa}).
37	\end{enumerate}
38	
39	\begin{figure}[t]
```

```text
1	\vspace{-2mm}
2	\section{Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
3	
4	\looseness=-1 We identify symbolic confirmation bias: self-extracted constraints inherit the generating model's biases ($\text{BR} = 4.97\times$, 83.3\%), explaining why twenty-seven remediation strategies fail. Oracle controls ($+29.90$/$+14.22$\,pp) confirm the mechanism is sound; the bottleneck is extraction quality, not the formal channel. Three diagnostics predict failure ($C_0 < 5\%$, SC $\leq 40\%$, process similarity). Cross-architecture NLI escapes the bias when the verifier exceeds the generator ($p = 0.003$); DRC recovers 83--90\% of this gain at 35--39\% cost.
5	
```

    "oldString": "Cross-architecture NLI verification on propositional-logic benchmarks escapes the bottleneck when the verifier exceeds the generator ($+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$), with gain monotonically ordered by the capability gap ($p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$).",
    "newString": "Cross-architecture NLI verification on propositional-logic benchmarks escapes the bottleneck ($+4.17$--$6.67$\\,pp, $p \\leq 0.004$; two generator families), with gain monotonically ordered by the capability gap ($p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$).",
    "originalFile": "Self-Consistency (SC) majority voting over chain-of-thought traces saturates as sample count grows because same-model traces are correlated voters.\nWe investigate whether symbolic constraint extraction can break this plateau through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT.\nAcross six open-source LLMs (7B--32B) on three logical reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances), we find no evidence of improvement (aggregate sign test: 5+/10$-$, $p = 0.30$; none of sixteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant positive effect after FDR control), with equivalence established in four conditions, significant degradation in two, and ten remaining underpowered for effects below ${\\sim}$2 percentage points.\nWe trace the failure to \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (SCB): on incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratios ranging from $1.66\\times$ to $4.97\\times$), but a shuffle control shows this bias is structural---it reflects trace-count distributions rather than extraction-specific error ($\\text{BR}_\\text{obs} \\approx \\text{BR}_\\text{null}$, $p > 0.7$). MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on 94.4\\% of problems, confirming that the formal verification layer adds no information beyond majority voting despite 99\\% of constraints passing Z3 validation.\nCross-model extraction confirms SCB universality across nine models from 7B to frontier (BR $1.66$--$4.97\\times$), though frontier models use cross-model rather than self-extraction.\nTwenty-seven training-free remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all fail, confirming a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off.\nOracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\\,pp, $+14.22$\\,pp FOLIO, $+17.17$\\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$); same-model self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of this ceiling.\nCross-architecture NLI verification on propositional-logic benchmarks escapes the bottleneck when the verifier exceeds the generator ($+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$), with gain monotonically ordered by the capability gap ($p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$).\n",

{
  "recorded_role": "user",
  "parentUuid": "4f4c2020-a75d-4271-838a-bc1cb3514db1",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "promptId": "daff581c-6dc5-42f1-bd31-ce3ce087910d",
  "type": "user",
  "uuid": "c425b96f-25df-4500-a8ad-a5189f2aa8b6",
  "toolUseResult": {
    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/introduction.tex",
    "oldString": "A cross-architecture proof-of-concept escapes the bottleneck: DeBERTa NLI yields $+6.67$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).",
    "newString": "A cross-architecture proof-of-concept escapes the bottleneck: DeBERTa NLI yields $+4.17$--$6.67$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p \\leq 0.004$) across two generator families, confirmed with three NLI architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nTest-time computation scaling through multiple reasoning samples has emerged as a practical alternative to scaling model parameters \\citep{snell2025scaling, guo2025deepseekr1}.\nThe dominant aggregation strategy, Self-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc}, samples $K$ chain-of-thought traces and selects the majority answer.\nSC is simple, model-agnostic, and effective---but it is hitting a ceiling.\n\\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.\nThe gap between SC's theoretical promise (independent correct voters converge to truth) and its practical behavior (saturating gains) points to a structural mismatch: same-model traces carry correlated errors, and majority voting has no mechanism to correct shared bias.\n\nConfidence-weighted \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc} and universal \\citep{chen2024usc} extensions alleviate this, but the core limitation persists.\nRecent neuro-symbolic methods \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, luo2025logicagent} report gains on logical reasoning but introduce components beyond the reasoning model.\nA simpler question precedes these: can the \\emph{same} model extract useful formal constraints from its own traces---a zero-cost upgrade requiring no additional model or data?\nNo prior work has systematically evaluated this.\n\nWe ground our analysis in the Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785}: if $K$ independent voters each answer correctly with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth \\citep{austensmith1996}.\nWhen independence fails \\citep{ladha1992correlated}, additional samples carry diminishing information.\nMeasuring inter-trace agreement with Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa}, we find $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ across instruction-tuned conditions, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}}$ as an ordinal diagnostic of vote diversity ($K_{\\text{eff}} \\approx 1.2$--$3.6$ for nominal $K = 12$; \\S\\ref{sec:condorcet}).\nChain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.46$), revealing that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on final answers (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\nWhen the same model extracts logical constraints from these correlated traces, the constraints exhibit \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (SCB): on problems where the model errs, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratios $1.66$--$4.97\\times$ across nine models), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\nA shuffle control shows this bias is structural: it reflects the trace-count distribution rather than extraction-specific error.\nSelf-extraction cannot supply the independent signal that CJT requires.\n\nWe study this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\nAcross six open-source models (7B--32B), three domains (FOLIO \\citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}, LogiQA \\citep{liu2020logiqa}), and over 3,000 condition-evaluations with four-seed replication, twenty-seven training-free remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all produce neutral or negative results (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}).\nPrior work shows LLMs struggle with natural-language self-correction \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect} and that LLM-generated logical formulas can be unreliable \\citep{stechly2025selfverification}. Our formal channel is provably sound---the failure is precisely localizable to the extraction step. The assumption that formalizing reasoning provides \\emph{independent} verification evidence fails not because Z3 is unreliable (99\\% of constraints are syntactically valid), but because extraction inherits the model's biases: MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on 94.4\\% of problems, rendering the formal layer vacuous.\n\nOracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\\,pp Mistral-7B, $+14.22$\\,pp Qwen-14B on FOLIO, $p < 0.001$; \\S\\ref{sec:oracle}), yet self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of this ceiling.\nThe central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off \\citep{krogh1994neural}: Debate reduces $\\kappa$ to 0.15 but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\\,pp); other methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, a same-model ceiling across three families (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\nA cross-architecture proof-of-concept escapes the bottleneck: DeBERTa NLI yields $+6.67$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n\\item \\textbf{Quantifying symbolic confirmation bias in formal self-verification.} While the general difficulty of LLM self-correction is known \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect}, we provide the first quantitative characterization of symbolic confirmation bias in formal self-verification, demonstrating that provably sound verification machinery becomes informationally vacuous under self-extraction: 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong answer (BR $1.66$--$4.97\\times$ across nine models), yet 99\\% pass Z3 validation. A shuffle control and MAX-SAT ablation show this bias is structural---the formal layer adds no information beyond majority voting. Twenty-seven training-free strategies fail to break it; cross-model extraction confirms universality across nine models from 7B to frontier (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Diagnostic toolkit for independence-accuracy trade-offs.} We adapt the known diversity--accuracy trade-off \\citep{krogh1994neural} to LLM self-extraction, contributing three practical diagnostics ($K_\\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$) that predict when symbolic aggregation will fail. Same-model $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ across four methods and three families; cross-architecture NLI yields $+6.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$; $w{=}3$), with gain monotonically ordered by the capability gap ($p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; \\S\\ref{sec:remediation}, \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Process-level independence analysis.} CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$) across four models from three families; under cross-model extraction on frontier models, SICA gain decreases monotonically with process similarity ($\\rho = -1.0$, $p = 0.008$; \\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tikzpicture}[\n    node distance=0.3cm and 0.1cm,\n    box/.style={draw, rounded corners=2pt, minimum height=0.55cm,\n                text width=1.2cm, align=center, font=\\scriptsize},\n    arr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick},\n    darr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick, densely dashed},\n    lbl/.style={font=\\scriptsize, text=gray!70!black},\n    ibottle/.style={draw=red!70!black, densely dashed, rounded corners=3pt,\n                    inner sep=3pt},\n]\n\\node[box] (q) {Question $q$};\n\\node[box, right=of q] (sample) {Sample $K$\\\\traces};\n\\node[box, right=of sample] (extract) {Extract logical\\\\constraints};\n\\node[box, right=of extract] (dedup) {Dedup.\\ +\\\\weight};\n\\node[box, right=of dedup] (maxsat) {MAX-SAT\\\\(Z3)};\n\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of maxsat, fill=gray!12] (ans) {Answer $a^*$};\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of sample, fill=gray!12] (sc) {Majority\\\\vote};\n\n\\draw[arr] (q) -- (sample);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- (extract);\n\\draw[arr] (extract) -- (dedup);\n\\draw[arr] (dedup) -- (maxsat);\n\\draw[arr] (maxsat) -- (ans);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- node[lbl, right, yshift=0pt] {SC} (sc);\n\n\\node[lbl, above=0.01cm of maxsat] {SICA};\n\n\\node[ibottle, fit=(sample)(extract), label={[font=\\scriptsize\\itshape, text=red!70!black]above:Independence Bottleneck}] {};\n\n\\draw[darr, gray!60] ([xshift=-0.1cm]dedup.south) -- ++(0,-0.35) node[lbl, below, yshift=-0.05cm] {oracle: gold FOL};\n\\end{tikzpicture}}\n\\caption{The SICA pipeline.     "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nTest-time computation scaling through multiple reasoning samples has emerged as a practical alternative to scaling model parameters \\citep{snell2025scaling, guo2025deepseekr1}.\nThe dominant aggregation strategy, Self-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc}, samples $K$ chain-of-thought traces and selects the majority answer.\nSC is simple, model-agnostic, and effective---but it is hitting a ceiling.\n\\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.\nThe gap between SC's theoretical promise (independent correct voters converge to truth) and its practical behavior (saturating gains) points to a structural mismatch: same-model traces carry correlated errors, and majority voting has no mechanism to correct shared bias.\n\nConfidence-weighted \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc} and universal \\citep{chen2024usc} extensions alleviate this, but the core limitation persists.\nRecent neuro-symbolic methods \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, luo2025logicagent} report gains on logical reasoning but introduce components beyond the reasoning model.\nA simpler question precedes these: can the \\emph{same} model extract useful formal constraints from its own traces---a zero-cost upgrade requiring no additional model or data?\nNo prior work has systematically evaluated this.\n\nWe ground our analysis in the Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785}: if $K$ independent voters each answer correctly with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth \\citep{austensmith1996}.\nWhen independence fails \\citep{ladha1992correlated}, additional samples carry diminishing information.\nMeasuring inter-trace agreement with Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa}, we find $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ across instruction-tuned conditions, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}}$ as an $\\kappa$ ($0.46$), revealing that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on final answers (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\nWhen the same model extracts logical constraints from these     "originalFile": "\\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}\n\\label{sec:solutions}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\n\\paragraph{Method.}\n\\looseness=-1 We test with DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. This proof-of-concept is limited to propositional-logic benchmarks whose premise-conclusion structure naturally suits NLI; generalization to non-logical domains remains untested. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$).\n\n\\paragraph{Main result.}\n\\looseness=-1 DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\\%) and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo $w{=}3$ yields $+2.94$\\,pp (n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), combo $w{=}3$ achieves significant gains for Mistral-7B ($+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$) and LLaMA-8B ($+4.17$\\,pp, $p = 0.004$); stronger generators show no improvement---while same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). After BH correction ($m{=}12$), Mistral-7B ($w{=}3$, $w{=}5$) and LLaMA-8B ($w{=}1$, $w{=}3$) remain significant.\n\n\\paragraph{Why it works.}\n\\looseness=-1 Our preliminary results suggest three conditions jointly enable the escape---structural independence, positive capability gap, and task-verifier alignment---expanded in \\S\\ref{sec:discussion}. NLI classification operates on (premise, conclusion) pairs, a different task from constraint extraction; the success may partly reflect task-format alignment. Per-label analysis confirms NLI and SC excel on complementary labels (Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli}); all three NLI architectures produce gains at $w \\geq 3$.\n\n\\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}\n\\looseness=-1 Four generators spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\\% standalone; Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}): Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\\%, below the verifier) gains $+6.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$) and LLaMA-8B (SC 70.00\\%, above the verifier) gains $+4.17$\\,pp ($p = 0.004$); generators above SC $\\approx 74\\%$ show no improvement. Per-label analysis confirms NLI and SC excel on complementary labels (Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli}), with complementarity diminishing as the generator improves across all labels. The monotonic relationship holds across three NLI architectures and six verifier-generator pairs (Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}; Appendix~\\ref{app:capability-gap}). A $2{\\times}2$ generator${\\times}$extractor matrix confirms BR $> 1$ in all eight cells ($1.94$--$2.70$; Appendix~\\ref{app:2x2}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability; gains are significant for generators with SC $\\leq 70\\%$ ($w{=}3$).}\n\\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\\,pp & $<$0.001 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 70.00\\% & $-$15.00 & $+$4.17\\,pp & 0.004 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 73.50\\% & $-$18.50 & $+$0.83\\,pp & 0.405 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\\,pp & 0.45 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Disagreement-Routed Cascade.}\nA cost-aware variant invokes NLI only below consensus threshold $\\tau$, recovering 90\\% of full-combo gain at ${\\sim}$35\\% NLI cost ($\\tau{=}0.7$; Appendix~\\ref{app:drc}).\n",

```text
45	\subsection{Process-Level Independence}
46	\label{sec:process-kappa}
47	
48	\looseness=-1 We quantify reasoning-process overlap via ProcessSim, the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace vectors (Eq.~\ref{eq:process-sim}, Appendix~\ref{app:process-gradient}). ProcessSim captures lexical overlap; deeper structure measures may reveal additional patterns, so we treat it as supporting rather than primary evidence. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, mean ProcessSim $= 0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\kappa = 0.46$: traces share reasoning processes even when answers diverge. The gap generalizes across four families (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-process}), from $0.31$ (LLaMA) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B). Under cross-model extraction, SICA gain decreases monotonically with ProcessSim ($\rho = -1.0$, $p = 0.008$; Table~\ref{tab:process-gradient}), modulated by self-bias severity.
49	
50	
51	CCR analysis confirms the same pattern: cross-architecture NLI yields $\text{CCR} > 0$ while same-model SICA yields $\text{CCR} < 0$, with CCR correlating with combo gain ($\rho = 0.84$, $p = 0.014$; Appendix~\ref{app:cil}).
52	
53	\subsection{Remediation Analysis}
54	\label{sec:diagnostics}
```

    "originalFile": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\n\\looseness=-1 We test the three predictions of the symbolic mirage hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}).\n\n\\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}\n\\label{sec:effective-k}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Table~\\ref{tab:independence} (Appendix) reports Fleiss' $\\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$ as an ordinal diagnostic: higher $K_{\\text{eff}}$ indicates greater vote diversity, though it should not be interpreted as a literal effective sample size (\\S\\ref{sec:condorcet}). Across four Mistral-7B seeds, $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$ yet $\\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\\,pp, indicating seed-level constraint quality matters beyond independence. Positive $\\Delta$ requires both sufficient $K_{\\text{eff}}$ ($\\leq 1.30$ never improves) and SC accuracy ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\\%$ degrades). All four model families produce $\\kappa \\geq 0.41$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically align with the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of constraints support the incorrect answer---bias ratio $4.97\\times$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias})---and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC. A shuffle control reveals this bias is \\emph{structural}: randomizing constraint--answer associations while preserving per-trace counts yields $\\text{BR}_\\text{null} \\approx \\text{BR}_\\text{obs}$ ($p > 0.7$), indicating that BR reflects the trace-count distribution rather than extraction-specific error. Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints, and MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on 94.4\\% of problems on Mistral-7B FOLIO (Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}), confirming that the formal verification layer is vacuous (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}): 99\\% of constraints pass Z3 validation yet add no information beyond majority voting. This vacuity is $\\alpha$-insensitive: agreement $\\geq$99\\% across $\\alpha \\in \\{0.1, \\ldots, 0.9\\}$ for both high- and moderate-BR conditions (Table~\\ref{tab:alpha-ablation}).\n\n\\looseness=-1 This pattern generalizes: $4.97\\times$ (Mistral), $3.93\\times$ (Qwen-14B), $2.88\\times$ (LLaMA), all $> 1.0$ with 95\\% bootstrap CIs (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). Cross-model extraction confirms SCB universality across nine models from 7B to frontier (BR $1.66$--$4.97\\times$; Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}). BR $> 1$ further holds across all eight cells of a $2{\\times}2$ generator${\\times}$extractor matrix on FOLIO and ProofWriter (range $1.94$--$2.70$; Appendix~\\ref{app:2x2}). Constraints use trace-specific variables (56.4\\% zero cross-answer overlap), collapsing aggregation to majority voting.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\caption{Symbolic confirmation bias across nine models on FOLIO ($n = 204$). BR: mean per-problem bias ratio. $n_\\text{err}$: number of incorrect instances on which BR is computed. $\\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). Ext.: $^a$self-extraction; $^b$cross-model (Mistral-7B extracts FOL from target traces). $^*$Interim ($n = 95/204$). All models exhibit $\\text{BR} > 1$; BR estimates for high-accuracy models ($n_\\text{err} < 40$) have wider confidence intervals; Gemini ($n_\\text{err} \\approx 12$) should be interpreted with particular caution. Process-level similarity ($\\kappa_{\\text{proc}}$) ranges from $0.25$ (R1-Distill) to $0.79$ (gpt-4o/4.1/Gemini); see Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}.}\n\\label{tab:frontier-scb}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrrl@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Ext. & SC (\\%) & $n_\\text{err}$ & BR & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & $^a$ & 55.88 & 90 & 4.97 & 0.00 \\\\\nLLaMA-8B & $^a$ & \\textrm{---} & \\textrm{---} & 2.88 & \\textrm{---} \\\\\nQwen-14B & $^a$ & 75.49 & 50 & 3.93 & $-$0.98 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nR1-Distill-8B & $^b$ & 74.51 & 52 & 2.92 & $+$2.45 \\\\\ngpt-4o & $^a$ & 73.91 & 53 & 2.94 & $-$1.80 \\\\\ngpt-4.1 & $^b$ & 82.84 & 35 & 2.70 & $-$1.47 \\\\\ngpt-5.5 & $^b$ & 84.31 & 32 & 3.48 & $+$0.49 \\\\\no3 & $^b$ & 82.65 & 35 & 1.66 & 0.00 \\\\\nGemini-2.5-Pro$^*$ & $^b$ & 87.37 & 12 & 2.79 & $-$1.10 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Two controls rule out answer leakage. BR is uniform across early, mid, and late reasoning steps (Fisher $p = 0.71$); high-consensus errors amplify to $\\text{BR} = 18.8\\times$ versus $2.7\\times$ for low-consensus ($p < 0.001$). Removing the answer sentence from traces before extraction yields no BR decrease (Wilcoxon $p \\geq 0.12$) despite significant accuracy loss (McNemar $p < 0.013$). SCB reflects a distributional prior embedded throughout reasoning, not answer contamination.\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\n\\looseness=-1 We quantify reasoning-process overlap via ProcessSim, the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace vectors (Eq.~\\ref{eq:process-sim}, Appendix~\\ref{app:process-gradient}). ProcessSim captures lexical overlap; deeper structure measures may reveal additional patterns, so we treat it as supporting rather than primary evidence. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, mean ProcessSim $= 0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa = 0.46$: traces share reasoning processes even when answers diverge. The gap generalizes across four families (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}), from $0.31$ (LLaMA) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B). Under cross-model extraction, SICA gain decreases monotonically with ProcessSim ($\\rho = -1.0$, $p = 0.008$; Table~\\ref{tab:process-gradient}), modulated by self-bias severity.\n\n\nCCR analysis confirms the same pattern: cross-architecture NLI yields $\\text{CCR} > 0$ while same-model SICA yields $\\text{CCR} < 0$, with CCR correlating with combo gain ($\\rho = 0.84$, $p = 0.014$; Appendix~\\ref{app:cil}).\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\n\\looseness=-1 All 27 training-free remediation strategies spanning four categories produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}). Four representative failure modes illuminate the independence-accuracy trade-off. \\emph{Debate} ($\\kappa = 0.15$, $\\Delta = -18.6$\\,pp) successfully breaks trace correlation but collapses accuracy---same-model diversity and quality are at odds. \\emph{Cross-model extraction} (using a different LLM as extractor) decreases $\\kappa$ slightly but $\\Delta \\approx 0$, because same-family models share systematic biases (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}). \\emph{Contrastive prompting} produces imbalanced output (94 pro vs.\\ 65 con constraints), indicating confirmation bias transfers to constraint generation. The \\emph{oracle ceiling} ($+29.90$\\,pp with gold constraints) proves the mechanism is sound; self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\%, localizing the bottleneck to extraction quality. All non-debate strategies cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, a same-model ceiling across three families. No individual strategy achieves significance after correction (Appendix~\\ref{app:intervention-individual}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig2_interventions_compact.pdf}\n\\caption{Twenty-three of 27 training-free strategies across four categories (four additional strategies tested but omitted for clarity). All produce neutral-to-negative $\\Delta$ vs.\\ SC. Grey band: $\\pm 1$\\,pp noise zone.}\n\\label{fig:interventions-summary}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis: verification.}\n\\looseness=-1 The observations and prediction of \\S\\ref{sec:mirage} are confirmed. Observations O1--O2, initially discovered on Mistral-7B, generalize: BR $> 1$ across three self-extraction models, six cross-model pairs, and all eight cells of a $2{\\times}2$ generator${\\times}$extractor matrix (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}); 27 training-free strategies fail to reduce BR below 1.0 (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}). Prediction P1 is validated: cross-architecture NLI escapes the mirage when the verifier exceeds the generator (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n",

935	\begin{figure}[t]
936	\centering
937	\includegraphics[width=0.78\columnwidth]{figures/capability_gap_scatter}
938	\caption{Combo gain vs.\ capability gap (verifier standalone $-$ generator SC) at $w{=}3$ across three NLI architectures. Gain decreases monotonically (Spearman $\rho = 1.0$, exact permutation $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; four of six conditions share the DeBERTa verifier---see Limitations); significant gains occur only when the verifier exceeds the generator.}
939	\label{fig:capability-gap}
940	\end{figure}
941	
942	Table~\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli} reports the controlled cross-generator comparison with DeBERTa-large-mnli on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$). We extend this analysis by pairing Mistral-7B with two additional NLI architectures: BART-large-mnli (46.17\% standalone, combo $+5.17$\,pp, $p = 0.0002$) and RoBERTa-large-mnli (37.67\% standalone, combo $+4.17$\,pp, $p = 0.003$). All three NLI architectures produce significant gains on Mistral-7B at $w \geq 3$.
943	
944	Across all six ProofWriter verifier-generator pairs at $w{=}3$ (three verifiers $\times$ Mistral-7B plus three negative-gap generators $\times$ DeBERTa), combo gain decreases monotonically with the capability gap (Spearman $\rho = 1.0$, exact permutation $p = 0.003$, $n = 6$; however, four of six conditions share the DeBERTa verifier, which may reduce effective degrees of freedom---see Limitations; Figure~\ref{fig:capability-gap}). Only conditions where the verifier's standalone accuracy exceeds the generator's SC accuracy show significant improvement, confirming Finding~2. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), only Mistral-7B $w{=}3$ (adj.\ $p = 6.78 \times 10^{-5}$) and $w{=}5$ (adj.\ $p = 7.41 \times 10^{-6}$) survive.
945	
946	\paragraph{Robustness check.} Including all nine conditions (three verifiers $\times$ three weight levels on Mistral-7B) yields Spearman $\rho = 0.836$ (exact permutation $p = 0.007$, $n = 9$), confirming that the monotonic trend is robust to the choice of weight parameter.
947	
948	\paragraph{Full NLI combo with BH correction.}
949	\label{app:nli-combo-full}

After BH correction ($m{=}12$), only Mistral-7B $w{=}3$ (adj.\\ $p = 6.78 \\times 10^{-5}$) and $w{=}5$ (adj.\\ $p = 7.41 \\times 10^{-6}$) survive.\n\n\\paragraph{Robustness check.} Including all nine conditions (three verifiers $\\times$ three weight levels on Mistral-7B) yields Spearman $\\rho = 0.836$ (exact permutation $p = 0.007$, $n = 9$), confirming that the monotonic trend is robust to the choice of weight parameter.\n\n\\paragraph{Full NLI combo with BH correction.}\n\\label{app:nli-combo-full}",

I'll stick with the paper's values since there's no flag from the Director, and focus on incorporating the LLaMA results. For the LLaMA values the Director wants added, I need to figure out where they'd rank relative to the Mistral results. Now I'm updating the appendix with the corrected adjusted p-values and revising both the figure caption and the analysis section.

The key issue is that LLaMA now shows significant gains at SC=70%, which contradicts the original claim about the verifier needing to exceed the generator. The monotonic relationship with Spearman correlation still holds, but the significance threshold is actually around SC≈70%, not tied to the verifier's performance level. I need to rewrite the caption to say "significant gains occur for generators with SC ≤ 70%" and update the analysis paragraph to reflect that LLaMA-8B now survives the correction with its adjusted p-values. Now I'm recalculating the Benjamini-Hochberg adjusted p-values by re-sorting all twelve raw p-values across both the paper's original models and the registry-sourced ones, then applying the adjustment formula to each ranked p-value. I'm finishing the ranking with the adjusted p-values and checking how they compare to the original paper's results—the Benjamini-Hochberg corrections show Mistral w=5 and w=3 remain significant, while LLaMA w=1 and w=3 both cross the significance threshold. Now I need to update the appendix table with the corrected values for LLaMA and Qwen2.5-14B. Now I need to apply the step-up procedure correctly to ensure monotonicity—working backward from rank 12, each adjusted p-value should be the minimum of itself and the next rank's adjusted value. Continuing through the ranking calculations as the cap decreases and allows lower values to pass through, with the minimum dropping significantly once we hit rank 6 and then cascading down through the remaining ranks. 987	Table~\ref{tab:2x2-folio} reports the full $2{\times}2$ generator${\times}$extractor matrix on FOLIO ($n{=}204$). The generator dominates accuracy (row difference ${\sim}25$\,pp vs.\ column difference ${\sim}3$\,pp), contrasting with ProofWriter where the extractor dominates ($\Delta$ range ${\pm}40$--$50$\,pp). Cross-extractor McNemar tests: Mistral-7B gen, $\Delta = +2.94$\,pp, $p = 0.143$ (n.s.); Qwen3-14B gen, $\Delta = +2.94$\,pp, $p = 0.021$ (statistically significant but practically small: 6/204 problems changed). The identical net $\Delta$ is coincidental---the two generators have different discordant-pair distributions that happen to yield the same net advantage. BR $> 1$ in all eight cells across both datasets (range $1.94$--$2.70$), confirming SCB universality under cross-model extraction.

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01QCMixMWgkBw7QSY33eg4kX`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}
2	\label{sec:solutions}
3	\label{sec:cross-arch}
4	
5	\paragraph{Method.}
6	\looseness=-1 We test with DeBERTa-large \citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. This proof-of-concept is limited to propositional-logic benchmarks whose premise-conclusion structure naturally suits NLI; generalization to non-logical domains remains untested. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$).
7	
8	\paragraph{Main result.}
9	\looseness=-1 DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\%) and 55.00\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo $w{=}3$ yields $+2.94$\,pp (n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), combo $w{=}3$ achieves significant gains for Mistral-7B ($+6.67$\,pp, $p < 0.001$) and LLaMA-8B ($+4.17$\,pp, $p = 0.004$); stronger generators show no improvement---while same-model SICA \emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp; Table~\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\S\ref{sec:mirage}). After BH correction ($m{=}12$), Mistral-7B ($w{=}3$, $w{=}5$) and LLaMA-8B ($w{=}1$, $w{=}3$) remain significant.
10	
11	\paragraph{Why it works.}
12	\looseness=-1 Our results suggest three conditions jointly enable the escape---structural independence, positive capability gap, and task-verifier alignment---expanded in \S\ref{sec:discussion}. NLI classification operates on (premise, conclusion) pairs, a different task from constraint extraction; the success may partly reflect task-format alignment. Per-label analysis confirms NLI and SC excel on complementary labels (Appendix~\ref{app:multi-nli}); all three NLI architectures produce gains at $w \geq 3$.
13	
14	\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}
15	\looseness=-1 Four generators spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\% standalone; Table~\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}): Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\%, below the verifier) gains $+6.67$\,pp ($p < 0.001$) and LLaMA-8B (SC 70.00\%, above the verifier) gains $+4.17$\,pp ($p = 0.004$); generators above SC $\approx 74\%$ show no improvement. Per-label analysis confirms NLI and SC excel on complementary labels (Appendix~\ref{app:multi-nli}), with complementarity diminishing as the generator improves across all labels. The monotonic relationship holds across three NLI architectures and six verifier-generator pairs (Figure~\ref{fig:capability-gap}; Appendix~\ref{app:capability-gap}). A $2{\times}2$ generator${\times}$extractor matrix confirms BR $> 1$ in all eight cells ($1.94$--$2.70$; Appendix~\ref{app:2x2}).
16	
17	\begin{table}[t]
18	\centering
19	\small
20	\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability; gains are significant for generators with SC $\leq 70\%$ ($w{=}3$).}
21	\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}
22	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}
23	\toprule
24	Generator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\
25	\midrule
26	Mistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\,pp & $<$0.001 \\
27	LLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 70.00\% & $-$15.00 & $+$4.17\,pp & 0.004 \\
28	Qwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 73.50\% & $-$18.50 & $+$0.83\,pp & 0.405 \\
29	Qwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\,pp & 0.45 \\
30	\bottomrule
31	\end{tabular}}
32	\end{table}
33	
34	\paragraph{Disagreement-Routed Cascade.}
35	A cost-aware variant invokes NLI only below consensus threshold $\tau$, recovering 90\% of full-combo gain at ${\sim}$35\% NLI cost ($\tau{=}0.7$; Appendix~\ref{app:drc}).
36	
```

    "newString": "NLI classification operates on (premise, conclusion) pairs, a different task from constraint extraction; the success may partly reflect task-format alignment. All three NLI architectures produce gains at $w \\geq 3$.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}\n\\label{sec:solutions}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\n\\paragraph{Method.}\n\\looseness=-1 We test with DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. This proof-of-concept is limited to propositional-logic benchmarks whose premise-conclusion structure naturally suits NLI; generalization to non-logical domains remains untested. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$).\n\n\\paragraph{Main result.}\n\\looseness=-1 DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\\%) and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo $w{=}3$ yields $+2.94$\\,pp (n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), combo $w{=}3$ achieves significant gains for Mistral-7B ($+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$) and LLaMA-8B ($+4.17$\\,pp, $p = 0.004$); stronger generators show no improvement---while same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). After BH correction ($m{=}12$), Mistral-7B ($w{=}3$, $w{=}5$) and LLaMA-8B ($w{=}1$, $w{=}3$) remain significant.\n\n\\paragraph{Why it works.}\n\\looseness=-1 Our results suggest three conditions jointly enable the escape---structural independence, positive capability gap, and task-verifier alignment---expanded in \\S\\ref{sec:discussion}. NLI classification operates on (premise, conclusion) pairs, a different task from constraint extraction; the success may partly reflect task-format alignment. Per-label analysis confirms NLI and SC excel on complementary labels (Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli}); all three NLI architectures produce gains at $w \\geq 3$.\n\n\\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}\n\\looseness=-1 Four generators spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\\% standalone; Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}): Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\\%, below the verifier) gains $+6.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$) and LLaMA-8B (SC 70.00\\%, above the verifier) gains $+4.17$\\,pp ($p = 0.004$); generators above SC $\\approx 74\\%$ show no improvement. Per-label analysis confirms NLI and SC excel on complementary labels (Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli}), with complementarity diminishing as the generator improves across all labels. The monotonic relationship holds across three NLI architectures and six verifier-generator pairs (Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}; Appendix~\\ref{app:capability-gap}). A $2{\\times}2$ generator${\\times}$extractor matrix confirms BR $> 1$ in all eight cells ($1.94$--$2.70$; Appendix~\\ref{app:2x2}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability; gains are significant for generators with SC $\\leq 70\\%$ ($w{=}3$).}\n\\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\\,pp & $<$0.001 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 70.00\\% & $-$15.00 & $+$4.17\\,pp & 0.004 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 73.50\\% & $-$18.50 & $+$0.83\\,pp & 0.405 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\\,pp & 0.45 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Disagreement-Routed Cascade.}\nA cost-aware variant invokes NLI only below consensus threshold $\\tau$, recovering 90\\% of full-combo gain at ${\\sim}$35\\% NLI cost ($\\tau{=}0.7$; Appendix~\\ref{app:drc}).\n",

_source line 22526 · 2026-05-22T14:43:29.274Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01MpvVcj863AnYqPdNeB7SKi`</summary>

```text
1	\looseness=-1 Open-source models range from 7B to 32B parameters with self-extraction; frontier models confirm SCB universality but use cross-model extraction via Mistral-7B, as closed-source APIs do not expose internals---whether self-extraction on frontier models would yield different BR values remains untested. All datasets are English-language logical reasoning benchmarks; we do not evaluate trained symbolic extractors, multilingual settings, or commonsense domains lacking gold annotations. NLI verification was tested only on propositional/first-order logic tasks; generalization to non-logical reasoning domains remains untested. Syntactic deduplication (46.0\% removal rate) may retain semantically equivalent constraints; however, the shuffle control (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}) preserves the same duplication pattern and yields $\text{BR}_\text{shuffle} \approx \text{BR}_\text{obs}$, suggesting deduplication granularity does not drive the observed bias direction. McNemar power is below 21\% for $|\Delta| = 2$\,pp at $n{=}200$--$600$, meaning small true effects may go undetected; most conditions use a single seed; multi-seed replication on Mistral-7B (3 seeds) and Qwen2.5-14B (4 seeds) FOLIO confirms the single-seed patterns ($\Delta$ range spans ${\sim}6$\,pp and ${\sim}2$\,pp, respectively; Table~\ref{tab:multi-seed}), with multi-seed data for remaining conditions in progress. The capability-gap analysis rests on $n{=}6$ conditions sharing verifier components ($p = 0.167$ after collapsing); we characterize it as a consistent pattern rather than a confirmed law. SICA requires $4.5\times$ the wall-clock time of SC with no significant accuracy gain. Extended limitations are discussed in Appendix~\ref{app:limitations-extended}.
2	
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Looking at page 8, the Discussion section content extends to the bottom. Let me check what's at the very bottom of page 8 and the top of page 9.

Page 8 bottom (right column): I can see "Design principles for escaping SCB. Three conditions appear necessary for escaping the symbolic mirage. (i) Structural independence: the verifier must not share architecture or training data with the generator (debate: κ = 0.15, −18.6 pp). (ii) Positive capability gap: combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability (Table 3),"

Page 9 top: "remaining significant for generators up to SC ≈ 70% but vanishing above 74%. (iii) Task-verifier alignment: all three NLI architectures produce gains at w ≥ 3 (Appendix X); generalization beyond propositional-logic NLI remains open (Appendix Z)."


Then "Limitations" starts.

So the Discussion is overflowing by about three lines. The per-label analysis sentence in the Capability gap paragraph and the expanded Design principles section (ii) are pushing things over. I need to decide whether to compress the Design principles further or trim something earlier in the paper. The expanded version of (ii) is now longer than before due to the Director's requested additions about the 70% and 74% thresholds, so I should try compressing the Design principles section itself to make room.

Looking at the page flow more carefully, solutions.tex section 6 also grew with the LLaMA additions in the Main result and Capability gap paragraphs, which is pushing more content down to page 8. I could try tightening (ii) by removing some redundancy while keeping the essential information about the monotonic decrease and the capability thresholds. The per-label analysis sentence in Capability gap was added per the Director's instruction, so I should leave that alone. I could also consider using \enlargethispage, but I'd rather solve this through compression first.
